### What are the fundamental differences between ionizing radiation (like X-rays) and non-ionizing radiation (like that used by 5G) in terms of their known effects on human biology?
Answer: Ionizing radiation like X-rays has sufficient photon energy to eject electrons from atoms and break chemical bonds, including those in DNA, which can cause mutations, cellular damage, and increase cancer risk even at relatively low doses. Non-ionizing radiation like the radio waves used by 5G lacks the photon energy to ionize atoms or break molecular bonds; its only well-established effect on human tissue at telecommunications power levels is negligible thermal heating. Answer: The key provisions of the Abortion Legislation Act (2020) include: abortion is available on request up to 20 weeks of gestation; after 20 weeks, abortion is permitted only if two health practitioners agree that it is clinically appropriate based on the woman's physical and mental health, wellbeing, and other relevant circumstances; the Act also decriminalizes abortion by removing it from the Crimes Act and facilitates access to abortion services by healthcare providers.

### Before the Abortion Legislation Act of 2020, what were the legal conditions and time limits for abortion in New Zealand under the previous law (the Contraception, Sterilisation, and Abortion Act 1977)?
Answer: Under the Contraception, Sterilisation, and Abortion Act 1977, abortion in New Zealand was technically a criminal offense under the Crimes Act 1961, but could be legally performed if two certifying consultants agreed that specific grounds were met. These grounds included serious danger to the life or physical or mental health of the pregnant woman, substantial risk of serious physical or mental disability of the fetus, and pregnancy resulting from incest or sexual violation. For pregnancies up to 20 weeks, these standard grounds applied. After 20 weeks gestation, abortion was only permitted in cases of serious danger to the woman's life or risk of serious permanent injury to her physical or mental health. The two-certifying-consultant requirement made the process more restrictive in practice than in many comparable countries.
